Around 315,100 hectares (1,217 sq mi), equivalent to 1% of Poland's territory, is protected within 23 [Polish national parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Poland), two of which – [Białowieża](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bia%C5%82owie%C5%BCa_Forest) and [Bieszczady](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bieszczady_National_Park) – are [UNESCO World Heritage Sites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Poland).[^cite_note-214] There are 123 areas designated as [landscape parks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Landscape_Park_\(Poland\)), along with numerous [nature reserves](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nature_reserve) and other [protected areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protected_areas_of_Poland) under the [Natura 2000](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Natura_2000) network.[^cite_note-215]&#x20;

## Government and politics

Main article: [Politics of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Politics_of_Poland)



[Karol Nawrocki](69581936.md)\
[President](43581.md)



[Donald Tusk](648724.md)\
[Prime Minister](40543.md)

Poland is a [unitary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unitary_state) [parliamentary republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliamentary_republic)[^cite_note-PARLIAMENTARY-5] and a [representative democracy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Representative_democracy), with [a president](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/President_of_the_Republic_of_Poland) as the [head of state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Head_of_state).[^cite_note-Serwis_Rzeczypospolitej_Polskiej-220] The executive power is exercised further by the [Council of Ministers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Council_of_Ministers_of_the_Republic_of_Poland) and the [prime minister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_the_Republic_of_Poland) who acts as the [head of government](37570.md).[^cite_note-Serwis_Rzeczypospolitej_Polskiej-220] The council's individual members are selected by the prime minister, approved by parliament and sworn in by the president.[^cite_note-Serwis_Rzeczypospolitej_Polskiej-220] The head of state is elected by [popular vote](652563.md) for a five-year term.[^cite_note-221] The current president is [Karol Nawrocki](69581936.md)[^cite_note-222] and the prime minister is [Donald Tusk](648724.md).&#x20;

Poland's [legislative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Legislature) assembly is a [bicameral](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bicameralism) parliament consisting of a 460-member lower house ([Sejm](28167.md)) and a 100-member upper house ([Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Senate_of_the_Republic_of_Poland)).[^cite_note-223] The Sejm is elected under [proportional representation](23298.md) according to the [d'Hondt method](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/D%27Hondt_method) for vote–seat conversion.[^cite_note-224] The Senate is elected under the [first-past-the-post](261709.md) electoral system, with one senator being returned from each of the one hundred constituencies.[^cite_note-Granat_2021-225] The Senate has the right to amend or reject a statute passed by the Sejm, but the Sejm may override the Senate's decision with a majority vote.[^cite_note-226]&#x20;



The [Sejm](28167.md) is the [lower house](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_house) of the [parliament of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Parliament_of_Poland).

With the exception of ethnic minority parties, only candidates of [political parties](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_political_parties_in_Poland) receiving at least 5% of the total national vote can enter the Sejm.[^cite_note-Granat_2021-225] Both the lower and upper houses of parliament in Poland are elected for a four-year term and each member of the Polish parliament is guaranteed [parliamentary immunity](320344.md).[^cite_note-Jasinski_2019-227] Under current legislation, a person must be 21 years of age or over to assume the position of deputy, 30 or over to become senator and 35 to run in a presidential election.[^cite_note-Jasinski_2019-227]&#x20;

Members of the Sejm and Senate jointly form the [National Assembly of the Republic of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_the_Republic_of_Poland).[^cite_note-Bien-Kacala_2021-228] The National Assembly, headed by the [marshal of the Sejm](1418762.md), or [marshal of the Senate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marshal_of_the_Senate) in their absence, is formed on three occasions – when a new president takes the [oath of office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oath_of_office); when an indictment against the president is brought to the [State Tribunal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Tribunal_\(Poland\)); and for a declaration of the president's permanent incapacity to exercise his duties due to the state of his health.[^cite_note-Bien-Kacala_2021-228]&#x20;

According to [International IDEA's](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/International_Institute_for_Democracy_and_Electoral_Assistance) Global State of Democracy (GSoD) Indices and Democracy Tracker, Poland performs in the mid-range on overall democratic measures, with particular weaknesses in civic engagement and judicial independence.[^cite_note-229][^cite_note-230][^cite_note-231]&#x20;

### Administrative divisions

Main article: [Administrative divisions of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Poland)

Poland is divided into 16 provinces or states known as [voivodeships](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voivodeships_of_Poland).[^cite_note-Teryt_2022-232] As of 2022, the voivodeships are subdivided into 380 counties (*[powiats](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powiat)*), which are further fragmented into 2,477 municipalities (*[gminas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gmina)*).[^cite_note-Teryt_2022-232] Major cities normally have the status of both *gmina* and *powiat*.[^cite_note-Teryt_2022-232] The provinces are largely founded on the borders of [historic regions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_historical_regions), or named for individual cities.[^cite_note-Marti-Henneberg_2021-233] Administrative authority at the voivodeship level is shared between a government-appointed governor ([voivode](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voivodes_of_Poland_\(since_1999\))), an elected regional assembly ([sejmik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voivodeship_sejmik)) and a [voivodeship marshal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Voivodeship_marshal), an executive elected by the assembly.[^cite_note-Marti-Henneberg_2021-233]&#x20;

### Law

Main article: [Law of Poland](4097680.md)



The [Constitution of 3 May](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_May_3,_1791) adopted in 1791 was the first modern constitution in Europe.

The [Constitution of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_Poland) is the enacted supreme law, and Polish judicature is based on the principle of civil rights, governed by the code of [civil law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Civil_law_\(legal_system\)).[^cite_note-235] The current democratic constitution was adopted by the [National Assembly of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Assembly_of_Poland) on 2 April 1997; it guarantees a [multi-party state](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multi-party_state) with freedoms of religion, speech and gatherings, prohibits the practices of forced [medical experimentation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medical_experimentation), torture or [corporal punishment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Corporal_punishment), and acknowledges the inviolability of the home, the right to form trade unions, and the right to [strike](155134.md).[^cite_note-236]&#x20;

The [judiciary](33696304.md) in Poland is composed of the [Supreme Court](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Supreme_Court_of_Poland) as the country's highest judicial organ, the [Supreme Administrative Court](2258359.md) for the judicial control of public administration, Common Courts ([District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/District_Court), [Regional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_Court_\(Poland\)), [Appellate](643.md)) and the [Military Court](58625.md).[^cite_note-237] The [Constitutional](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitutional_Tribunal_\(Poland\)) and State Tribunals are separate judicial bodies, which rule the constitutional liability of people holding the highest offices of state and supervise the compliance of [statutory law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Statutory_law), thus protecting the Constitution.[^cite_note-Jaremba_2013-238] Judges are nominated by the [National Council of the Judiciary](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Council_of_the_Judiciary) and are appointed for life by the [president](43581.md).[^cite_note-Jaremba_2013-238] With the approval of the Senate, the Sejm appoints an [ombudsman](26289198.md) for a five-year term to guard the observance of social justice.[^cite_note-Granat_2021-225]&#x20;

Poland has a low [homicide](123451.md) rate at 0.7 murders per 100,000 people, as of 2018.[^cite_note-\:0-239] Rape, assault and violent crime remain at a very low level.[^cite_note-240] The country has imposed strict regulations on [abortion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Abortion_in_Poland), which is permitted only in cases of [rape](7980471.md), [incest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Incest) or when the woman's life is in danger; [congenital disorder](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Congenital_disorder) is not covered by the law, prompting some women to seek abortion abroad.[^cite_note-241]&#x20;

Historically, the most significant Polish legal act is the [Constitution of 3 May 1791](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constitution_of_3_May_1791). Instituted to redress long-standing political defects of the [federative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Federation) [Polish–Lithuanian Commonwealth](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish%E2%80%93Lithuanian_Commonwealth) and its [Golden Liberty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Golden_Liberty), it was the first modern constitution in Europe and influenced many later [democratic movements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Democratic_movement) across the globe.[^cite_note-Davies_1996-242][^cite_note-243] In 1918, the [Second Polish Republic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_Polish_Republic) became one of the first countries to introduce universal [women's suffrage](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Women%27s_suffrage).[^cite_note-244]&#x20;

### Foreign relations

Main articles: [Foreign relations of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Poland) and [List of diplomatic missions of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_diplomatic_missions_of_Poland)



The [Ministry of Foreign Affairs](1161355.md), located in [Warsaw](32908.md)

Poland is a [middle power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Middle_power) and a [regional power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_power) in Europe.[^cite_note-245][^cite_note-246] It has a total of 53 representatives in the [European Parliament](9581.md) as of 2024. [Warsaw](32908.md) serves as the headquarters for [Frontex](3072700.md), the European Union's agency for external border security as well as [ODIHR](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/ODIHR), one of the principal institutions of the [OSCE](22796.md).[^cite_note-247][^cite_note-248] Apart from the European Union, Poland has been a member of [NATO](21133.md), the United Nations, the [World Trade Organization](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Trade_Organization) and regional alliances such as the [Visegrád Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group), the [Weimar Triangle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Weimar_Triangle) and the Polish-led [Three Seas Initiative](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Seas_Initiative).&#x20;

In recent years, Poland significantly strengthened its [relations](10858944.md) with the United States, becoming one of its closest [allies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alliance) and strategic partners in Europe.[^cite_note-249] Historically, Poland maintained strong cultural and political ties with [Hungary](20026103.md) and [France](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/France%E2%80%93Poland_relations).[^cite_note-250][^cite_note-251]&#x20;

### Military

Main article: [Polish Armed Forces](40153898.md)



[Polish Air Force](602835.md) [F-16s](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/F-16_Fighting_Falcon), a single-engine [multirole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Multirole_combat_aircraft) [fighter aircraft](10929.md)



The [amphibious](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Amphibious_vehicle) [infantry fighting vehicle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infantry_fighting_vehicle) [Borsuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borsuk_\(infantry_fighting_vehicle\))

The Polish Armed Forces are composed of five branches – the [Land Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Land_Forces), the [Navy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Navy), the [Air Force](602835.md), the [Special Forces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Special_forces_of_Poland) and the [Territorial Defence Force](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Territorial_Defence_Force_\(Poland\)).[^cite_note-Mihalcova_2019-252] The military is subordinate to the [Ministry of National Defence of the Republic of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Defence_of_the_Republic_of_Poland).[^cite_note-Mihalcova_2019-252] However, its commander-in-chief in peacetime is the president, who nominates officers, the Minister for National Defence and the chief of staff.[^cite_note-Mihalcova_2019-252] Polish military tradition is generally commemorated by the [Armed Forces Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Armed_Forces_Day_\(Poland\)), celebrated annually on 15 August.[^cite_note-253] In July 2024, the Polish Armed Forces had a combined strength of 216,100 active soldiers, making it the largest standing army in the European Union and the third largest in [NATO](21133.md).[^cite_note-Celej-254]&#x20;

Poland ranks [14th in the world](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_with_highest_military_expenditures) in terms of military expenditures; the country allocated 4.12% of its total GDP on military spending, equivalent to approximately US\$35 billion in 2024.[^cite_note-ENR-255] From 2022, Poland initiated a programme of mass modernisation of its armed forces, in close cooperation with American, South Korean and local Polish [defence manufacturers](608296.md)[^cite_note-256] concentrated in the [Polish Armaments Group](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Armaments_Group). Also, the Polish military is set to increase its size to 250,000 enlisted and officers, and 50,000 Territorial Defence Force.[^cite_note-257] According to [SIPRI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/SIPRI), the country exported €487 million worth of arms and armaments to foreign countries in 2020.[^cite_note-258]&#x20;

Compulsory [military service](5735.md) for men, was discontinued in 2008.[^cite_note-Day_2008-259] Polish military doctrine reflects the same defensive nature as that of its NATO partners and the country actively hosts NATO's [military exercises](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Military_exercises).[^cite_note-IISS_2022-260] The Polish Special Forces, consisting of six units ([Grom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/GROM_Military_Unit), [Formoza](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Formoza_Military_Unit), [Agat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agat_Military_Unit), [Nil](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nil_Military_Unit), [Commandos Military Unit](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commandos_Military_Unit), [Airborne Special Operations Unit](https://pl.wikipedia.org/wiki/Powietrzna_Jednostka_Operacji_Specjalnych)), have the authority to command full-scale as well as joint NATO operations, with special forces from all NATO member states.[^cite_note-261] Since 1953, the country has been a large contributor to various United Nations peacekeeping missions,[^cite_note-262] and currently maintains military presence in the Middle East, Africa, the [Baltic states](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baltic_states) and southeastern Europe.[^cite_note-IISS_2022-260] Moreover, there are approximately 10,000 troops from the [United States Armed Forces](32212.md) stationed across Poland.[^cite_note-DepartmentofState-263] From 2024, Poland also provides mandatory [firearms training](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gun_safety) for pupils in [primary](25058.md) and [secondary schools](554992.md).[^cite_note-PAPpl-264]&#x20;

### Security, law enforcement and emergency services



A [Toyota Auris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Toyota_Auris) patrol car belonging to the Polish [State Police Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Policja) (*Policja*)

Main articles: [Law enforcement in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Poland), [Emergency medical services in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services_in_Poland), and [State Fire Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Fire_Service)

Law enforcement in Poland is performed by several agencies which are subordinate to the [Ministry of Interior and Administration](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_and_Administration_\(Poland\)) – the [State Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Policja) (*Policja*), assigned to investigate crimes or transgression; the [Municipal City Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/City_Guard_\(Poland\)), which maintains [public order](3905736.md); and several specialised agencies, such as the [Polish Border Guard](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Border_Guard).[^cite_note-Narodowego_2013-265] Municipal guards are primarily headed by provincial, regional or city councils; individual guards are not permitted to carry [firearms](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Firearms) unless instructed by the superior commanding officer.[^cite_note-266] Security service personnel conduct regular patrols in both large urban areas or smaller suburban localities.[^cite_note-267]&#x20;

Emergency services in Poland consist of the [emergency medical services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services_in_Poland), [search and rescue](172599.md#Poland) units of the [Polish Armed Forces](40153898.md) and [State Fire Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/State_Fire_Service). Emergency medical services in Poland are operated by local and regional governments,[^cite_note-268] but are a part of the centralised national agency – the [National Medical Emergency Service](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Emergency_medical_services_in_Poland) (*Państwowe Ratownictwo Medyczne*).[^cite_note-269] Thanks to its location, Poland is a country essentially free from the threat of natural disasters such as [earthquakes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Earthquakes), [volcanic eruptions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volcanic_eruptions), [tornadoes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tornadoes) and [tropical cyclones](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tropical_cyclones). However, [floods](50482.md) have occurred in low-lying areas from time to time during periods of extreme rainfall, for example during the [2010 Central European floods](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2010_Central_European_floods).&#x20;

The [Internal Security Agency](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Internal_Security_Agency) (ABW, or ISA in English) is the chief [counterintelligence instrument](81014.md) safeguarding Poland's internal security, along with [Agencja Wywiadu](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agencja_Wywiadu) (AW) which identifies threats and collects secret information abroad.[^cite_note-270] The [Central Investigation Bureau of Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centralne_Biuro_%C5%9Aledcze_Policji) (CBŚP) and the [Central Anticorruption Bureau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Anticorruption_Bureau) (CBA) are responsible for countering organised crime and corruption in state and private institutions.[^cite_note-271][^cite_note-272]&#x20;

## Economy

Main article: [Economy of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Poland)

| [GDP (PPP)](12594.md) | \$2.164 trillion (2026)[^cite_note-IMFWEO.PL-14] |
| --- | --- |
| Nominal GDP | \$1.134 trillion (2026)[^cite_note-IMFWEO.PL-14] |
| Real GDP growth | 3.2% (2025)[^cite_note-273] |
| [CPI](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Consumer_price_index) inflation | 2.5% (May 2024)[^cite_note-274] |
| [Employment-to-population](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Employment-to-population_ratio) | 57% (2022)[^cite_note-275] |
| Unemployment | 3.3% (2026)[^cite_note-IMFWEO.PL-14] |
| [Total public debt](695460.md) | \$502.3 billion (2024)[^cite_note-276] |

Poland has a [social market economy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Social_market_economy) and is a regional [economic power](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economic_power) in [East-Central Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East-Central_Europe).[^cite_note-Kochanski\&Partners-277] As of 2025, the country's gross domestic product (GDP) is the sixth largest in the European Union by [nominal standards](1598533.md), the fifth largest by [purchasing power parity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_in_Europe_by_GDP_\(PPP\)), and ranks [20th globally](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_\(nominal\)).[^cite_note-278] It is one of the fastest growing within the Union and reached a [developed market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Developed_market) status in 2018.[^cite_note-279] The unemployment rate published by [Eurostat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurostat) in 2023 amounted to 2.8%, which was the second-lowest in the EU.[^cite_note-Ministry_of_Family_and_Social_Policy-280] As of 2023, around 62% of the employed population works in the [service sector](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tertiary_sector_of_the_economy), 29% in manufacturing, and 8% in the agricultural sector, thus manifesting a highly [diversified economy](63252200.md).[^cite_note-281] Although Poland is a member of the [European single market](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_single_market), the country has not adopted the [Euro](9472.md) as legal tender and maintains its own currency – the [Polish złoty](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_z%C5%82oty) (zł, PLN).[^cite_note-IM-282]&#x20;



[Gdańsk](12099.md), located on the [Baltic](3335.md) in the north of Poland, is a major [seaport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Seaport) as well as an economic and tourist zone.

Poland is a regional European leader in terms of [foreign direct investment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment)[^cite_note-wb-283] and possesses around 40 percent of the 500 biggest companies in the region by revenues whilst maintaining a [high globalisation rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Globalisation_index) and relatively high economic competitiveness.[^cite_note-Polish_economy_seen_as_stable_and_competitive-284][^cite_note-IMD-285] The country's largest firms compose the [WIG20](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIG20) and [WIG30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/WIG30) [stock market indexes](23408957.md), which are traded on the [Warsaw Stock Exchange](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Stock_Exchange).[^cite_note-Zarzecki\&Dietl-286] The [Central Statistical Office](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Central_Statistical_Office_\(Poland\)) estimated that in 2014 there were 1,437 Polish corporations with interests in 3,194 foreign entities.[^cite_note-287] Poland also has the largest banking sector in [East-Central Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/East-Central_Europe),[^cite_note-288] with 32.3 branches per 100,000 adults.[^cite_note-289] The monetary policy is determined by the [National Bank of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Bank_of_Poland) (NBP), which controls the issuing of the national currency.[^cite_note-IM-282] It was the only European economy to have avoided the [recession of 2008](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Great_Recession).[^cite_note-Schwab_2011-290] Since 2019, workers under the age of 26 are exempt from paying the [income tax](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Income_tax).[^cite_note-291]&#x20;

The country is the [19th largest exporter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_exports) of goods and [services](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_service_exports) in the world.[^cite_note-292] Exports of goods and services are valued at approximately 58% of GDP, as of 2023.[^cite_note-293] Poland's largest trade partners are Germany, the United Kingdom, the [Czech Republic](5321.md), France, Italy, the [Netherlands](21148.md), and the [United States](3434750.md).[^cite_note-OEC-294] Among its lead exports are motor cars, buses, and vehicle-related accessories, machinery, [electronics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electronics), [electric batteries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Electric_batteries), [home appliances](185982.md), furniture, cosmetics, military equipment, and [tobacco](30942.md) as well as materials such as silver, [copper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copper), [steel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Steel), [coal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_coal_production), [zinc](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zinc), [tar](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tar), and [coke](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coke_\(fuel\)).[^cite_note-OEC-294] In 2023, the country produced 1300 tonnes of silver and was the 5th largest silver producer globally.[^cite_note-295] As of May 2026, Poland holds the world's 11th largest [gold reserve](1056205.md), estimated at 582 tonnes.[^cite_note-296]&#x20;

### Tourism

Main article: [Tourism in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism_in_Poland)



&#x20;[Malbork Castle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malbork_Castle) is the largest castle in the world measured by land area and a [UNESCO World Heritage Site](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Poland).

In 2020, the total value of the [tourism industry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tourism) in Poland was 104.3 billion [PLN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_z%C5%82oty), then equivalent to 4.5% of the Polish GDP.[^cite_note-oecdtourism-297] Tourism contributes considerably to the overall economy and makes up a relatively large proportion of the country's service market.[^cite_note-298] Nearly 200,000 people were employed in the [accommodation and catering](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospitality_industry) (hospitality) sector in 2020.[^cite_note-oecdtourism-297] In 2021, Poland ranked [12th most visited country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World_Tourism_rankings) in the world by international arrivals.[^cite_note-unwto-299]&#x20;

Tourist attractions in Poland range from the mountains in the south to the beaches on the [Baltic Sea](3335.md) in the north. Many trail of rich architectural and cultural heritage. Among the most recognisable landmarks are Old Towns in [Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w_Old_Town), [Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Old_Town), [Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw) ([dwarf statues](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw%27s_dwarfs)), [Gdańsk](12099.md), [Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84), [Lublin](74594.md), [Toruń](31258.md) and [Zamość](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zamo%C5%9B%C4%87) as well as museums, zoological gardens, theme parks and the [Wieliczka Salt Mine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wieliczka_Salt_Mine), with its labyrinthine tunnels, [underground lake](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Underground_lake) and chapels carved by miners out of [rock salt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rock_salt) beneath the ground. There are [over 100 castles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_castles_in_Poland) in the country, largely within the [Lower Silesian Voivodeship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lower_Silesian_Voivodeship) (including the Piast Castles Trail), and also on the [Trail of the Eagles' Nests](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Trail_of_the_Eagles%27_Nests); the largest castle in the world by land area is situated in [Malbork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malbork_Castle).[^cite_note-300][^cite_note-301] The German [Auschwitz concentration camp](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Auschwitz_concentration_camp) in [Oświęcim](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/O%C5%9Bwi%C4%99cim), and the [Skull Chapel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Skull_Chapel) in [Kudowa-Zdrój](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kudowa-Zdr%C3%B3j) constitute [dark tourism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dark_tourism).[^cite_note-Watts-302] Regarding nature based travel, notable sites include the [Masurian Lake District](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masurian_Lake_District) and [Białowieża Forest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bia%C5%82owie%C5%BCa_Forest) in the east; on the south [Karkonosze](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karkonosze), the [Table Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Table_Mountains), the [Pieniny](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pieniny) and the [Tatra Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatra_Mountains), where [Rysy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rysy) and the [Eagle's Path](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Orla_Per%C4%87) trail are located. The [Bieszczady Mountains](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bieszczady_Mountains) lie in the extreme south-east.[^cite_note-UNTWO_2008-303]&#x20;

### Transport

Main article: [Transport in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Transport_in_Poland)



[PKP Intercity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_State_Railways) [Pendolino](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PKP_class_ED250) at [Wrocław Główny railway station](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw_G%C5%82%C3%B3wny_railway_station)

Transport in Poland is provided by means of [rail](13011368.md), [road](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highways_in_Poland), [marine shipping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Merchant_Navy) and [air travel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_airports_in_Poland). The country is part of EU's [Schengen Area](16565139.md) and is an important transport hub due to its strategic geographical position in Central Europe.[^cite_note-304] Some of the longest European routes, including the [E30](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_route_E30) and [E40](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/European_route_E40), run through Poland. The country has a good network of [highways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Highways_in_Poland) consisting of [express roads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Limited-access_road) and [motorways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Controlled-access_highway). As of August 2023, Poland has the world's [21st-largest road network](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_road_network_size), maintaining over 5,000 km (3,100 mi) of highways in use.[^cite_note-305] In larger cities, [public transport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Public_transport) is heavily utilised; Poland is one of Europe's largest bus and tram manufacturers, and hosts one of the biggest [tram and light rail transit systems](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_largest_tram_and_light_rail_transit_systems_ever).[^cite_note-Transport_and_Climate_Change-306]&#x20;

In 2022, the nation had 19,393 kilometres (12,050 mi) of railway track, the third longest in the European Union after Germany and France.[^cite_note-307] [Polish State Railways](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_State_Railways) (PKP) is the dominant railway operator, with certain major voivodeships or urban areas possessing their own [commuter](6286.md) and [regional rail](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Regional_rail).[^cite_note-308] Poland has a number of international airports, the largest of which is [Warsaw Chopin Airport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Chopin_Airport).[^cite_note-309] It is the primary global hub for [LOT Polish Airlines](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/LOT_Polish_Airlines), the country's [flag carrier](465816.md).[^cite_note-310]&#x20;

Seaports exist all along Poland's Baltic coast, with most freight operations using [Świnoujście](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_%C5%9Awinouj%C5%9Bcie), [Police](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Police), [Szczecin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Szczecin), [Kołobrzeg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Ko%C5%82obrzeg), [Gdynia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Gdynia), [Gdańsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Gda%C5%84sk) and [Elbląg](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Elbl%C4%85g) as their base. The [Port of Gdańsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Port_of_Gda%C5%84sk) is the only port in the [Baltic Sea](3335.md) adapted to receive oceanic vessels. [Polferries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polferries) and [Unity Line](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Unity_Line) are the largest Polish ferry operators, with the latter providing [roll-on/roll-off](1457484.md) and [train ferry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Train_ferry) services to [Scandinavia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Scandinavia).[^cite_note-311]&#x20;

### Energy

Main article: [Energy in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Poland)



Share of electricity generation in Poland - percentage share

The electricity generation sector in Poland is largely [fossil-fuel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fossil_fuel)–based. Coal production in Poland is a major source of employment and the largest source of the nation's [greenhouse gas emissions](9528025.md).[^cite_note-International_Energy_Agency_2022-312] Many power plants nationwide use Poland's position as a major European exporter of coal to their advantage by continuing to use coal as the primary raw material in the production of their energy. The three largest Polish coal mining firms ([Węglokoks](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C4%99glokoks), [Kompania Węglowa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kompania_W%C4%99glowa) and [JSW](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jastrz%C4%99bska_Sp%C3%B3%C5%82ka_W%C4%99glowa)) extract around 100 million tonnes of coal annually.[^cite_note-313] After coal, Polish energy supply relies significantly on [natural gas](22131.md) and oil.&#x20;

The new [Energy Policy of Poland until 2040](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Energy_in_Poland) (EPP2040) would reduce the share of coal and [lignite](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lignite) in electricity generation by 25% from 2017 to 2030. The plan involves deploying new nuclear plants, increasing energy efficiency, and decarbonising the Polish transport system in order to reduce greenhouse gas emissions and prioritise long-term energy security.[^cite_note-International_Energy_Agency_2022-312][^cite_note-314] In March 2026, [PEJ](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polskie_Elektrownie_J%C4%85drowe) submitted an application for a permit to build a nuclear power plant.&#x20;

### Science and technology

Main articles: [Timeline of Polish science and technology](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Timeline_of_Polish_science_and_technology) and [List of Polish Nobel laureates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Polish_Nobel_laureates)

Marie Curie

Physicist and chemist [Maria Skłodowska-Curie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sk%C5%82odowska-Curie) was the first person to win two [Nobel Prizes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nobel_Prizes).

[^cite_note-Mould_1993-315]

Nicolaus Copernicus

Astronomer [Nicolaus Copernicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolaus_Copernicus) formulated the [heliocentric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliocentrism) model of the [Solar System](26903.md).

Over the course of history, the Polish people have made considerable contributions in the fields of science, technology and mathematics.[^cite_note-316] Perhaps the most renowned Pole to support this theory was [Nicolaus Copernicus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicolaus_Copernicus), who triggered the [Copernican Revolution](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Copernican_Revolution) by placing the [Sun rather than the Earth at the centre of the universe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heliocentrism).[^cite_note-317] He also derived a [quantity theory of money](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quantity_theory_of_money), which made him a pioneer of economics. Copernicus' achievements and discoveries are considered the basis of Polish culture and cultural identity.[^cite_note-318] Poland was ranked 39th in the [Global Innovation Index](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index) in 2025.[^cite_note-319][^cite_note-320]&#x20;

Poland's tertiary education institutions; traditional [universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_universities_in_Poland), as well as technical, medical, and economic institutions, employ around tens of thousands of researchers and staff members. There are hundreds of research and development institutes.[^cite_note-321] However, in the 19th and 20th centuries many Polish scientists worked abroad; one of the most important of these exiles was [Maria Skłodowska-Curie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maria_Sk%C5%82odowska-Curie), a physicist and chemist who lived much of her life in France. In 1925, she established Poland's [Radium Institute](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Curie_Institute_\(Warsaw\)).[^cite_note-Mould_1993-315]&#x20;

In the first half of the 20th century, Poland was a flourishing centre of mathematics. Outstanding Polish mathematicians formed the [Lwów School of Mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lw%C3%B3w_School_of_Mathematics) (with [Stefan Banach](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stefan_Banach), [Stanisław Mazur](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Mazur), [Hugo Steinhaus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hugo_Steinhaus), [Stanisław Ulam](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Ulam)) and [Warsaw School of Mathematics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_School_of_Mathematics) (with [Alfred Tarski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Tarski), [Kazimierz Kuratowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazimierz_Kuratowski), [Wacław Sierpiński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wac%C5%82aw_Sierpi%C5%84ski) and [Antoni Zygmund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Antoni_Zygmund)). Numerous mathematicians, scientists, chemists or economists emigrated due to historic vicissitudes, among them [Benoit Mandelbrot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Benoit_Mandelbrot), [Leonid Hurwicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonid_Hurwicz), [Alfred Tarski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alfred_Tarski), [Joseph Rotblat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Rotblat) and Nobel Prize laureates [Roald Hoffmann](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roald_Hoffmann), [Georges Charpak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Georges_Charpak) and [Tadeusz Reichstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tadeusz_Reichstein).&#x20;

## Demographics

Main articles: [Demographics of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Demographics_of_Poland), [List of cities and towns in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Poland), [Metropolitan areas in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metropolitan_areas_in_Poland), [Polish people](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_people), and [Polish diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_diaspora)



[Warsaw](32908.md) is the largest [city in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_in_Poland), and an [economic hub of the country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Economy_of_Poland).

Poland has a population of approximately 38.2 million as of 2021, and is the [ninth-most populous country](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_European_countries_by_population) in Europe, as well as the fifth-most populous member state of the [European Union](9317.md).[^cite_note-322] It has a population density of 122 inhabitants per square kilometre (320 inhabitants/mi<sup>2</sup>).[^cite_note-323] The [total fertility rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Total_fertility_rate) was estimated at 1.2 children born to a woman in 2023, which is [among the world's lowest](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_sovereign_states_and_dependencies_by_total_fertility_rate).[^cite_note-324] Furthermore, Poland's population is [aging significantly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_countries_by_median_age), and the country has a [median age](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Median_age) of 42.2.[^cite_note-325]&#x20;

Around 60% of the country's population lives in urban areas or major cities and 40% in rural zones.[^cite_note-326] In 2020, 50.2% of Poles resided in [detached dwellings](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Detached_dwelling) and 44.3% in apartments.[^cite_note-327] The most populous administrative province or state is the [Masovian Voivodeship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Masovian_Voivodeship) and the most populous city is the capital, [Warsaw](32908.md), at 1.8 million inhabitants with a further 2–3 million people living in its [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_metropolitan_area).[^cite_note-328][^cite_note-329][^cite_note-330] The [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice_urban_area) of [Katowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice) is the largest urban [conurbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conurbation) with a population between 2.7 million[^cite_note-331] and 5.3 million residents.[^cite_note-332] Population density is higher in the south of Poland and mostly concentrated in [Upper Silesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Silesia), between the cities of [Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw) and [Kraków](16815.md).[^cite_note-333]&#x20;



The [metropolitan area](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice_urban_area) of [Katowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice) is the largest urban [conurbation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Conurbation), with [Upper Silesia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Upper_Silesia) being the most densely populated region in Poland.

In the [2011 Polish census](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_census_of_2011), 37,310,341 people reported [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_people) identity, 846,719 [Silesian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Silesians), 232,547 [Kashubian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashubians) and 147,814 [German](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/German_minority_in_Poland). Other [identities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_people#National_minorities) were reported by 163,363 people (0.41%) and 521,470 people (1.35%) did not specify any nationality.[^cite_note-Central_Statistical_Office_2015-334] Official population statistics do not include migrant workers who do not possess a permanent residency permit or [Karta Polaka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karta_Polaka).[^cite_note-335] More than 1.7 million [Ukrainian](145144.md) citizens worked legally in Poland in 2017.[^cite_note-336] The number of migrants is rising steadily; the country approved 504,172 work permits for foreigners in 2021 alone.[^cite_note-337]&#x20;

According to the 2021 census, ethnic Poles comprise 98.84% of the population, including people who declared Polish heritage alone (96.28%) or both Polish and another ethnicity (2.56%) as responders were allowed to select up to two ethnicities. People who declared only non-Polish ethnicities made up 1.13% of the population and people who did not report their ethnicity numbered 0.03%. The province with the highest percentage of ethnic Poles was the [Holy Cross Voivodeship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Cross_Voivodeship) (99.70%), and the region with the lowest share of ethnic Poles was the [Silesian Voivodeship](28969.md) (95.49%).[^cite_note-338]&#x20;
