### Languages

Main articles: [Polish language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language), [Languages of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_Poland), and [Bilingual communes in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilingual_communes_in_Poland)



*[Dolina Jadwigi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dolina_Jadwigi)*—a [bilingual](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilingual) Polish-[Kashubian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashubian_language) road sign with the village name

[Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) is the [official](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Official_language) and predominant spoken language in Poland, and is one of the official [languages of the European Union](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Languages_of_the_European_Union).[^cite_note-340] It is also a [second language](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Second_language) in parts of neighbouring [Lithuania](17675.md), where it is taught in Polish-minority schools.[^cite_note-341][^cite_note-342] Contemporary Poland is a linguistically [homogeneous](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Homogeneous) nation, with 97% of respondents declaring Polish as their mother tongue.[^cite_note-343] There are currently 15 minority languages in Poland,[^cite_note-344] including one recognised regional language, [Kashubian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashubian_language), which is spoken by approximately 100,000 people on a daily basis in the northern regions of [Kashubia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kashubia) and [Pomerania](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pomerania).[^cite_note-345] Poland also recognises [secondary administrative languages or auxiliary languages in bilingual municipalities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bilingual_communes_in_Poland), where bilingual signs and placenames are commonplace.[^cite_note-346] According to the [Centre for Public Opinion Research](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Centre_for_Public_Opinion_Research), around 32% of Polish citizens declared knowledge of the English language in 2015.[^cite_note-347]&#x20;

### Religion

Main article: [Religion in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Poland)



[John Paul II](23805.md), born Karol Wojtyła, held the papacy between 1978 and 2005 and was the first [Pole](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_people) to become [Pope](23056.md).

According to the 2021 census, 71.3% of all Polish citizens adhere to the [Catholic Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Poland), with 6.9% identifying as having no religion and 20.6% refusing to answer.[^cite_note-Census_2021-4]&#x20;

Poland is one of the [most religious countries in Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Religion_in_Europe), where Catholicism remains a part of national identity and Polish-born [Pope John Paul II](23805.md) is widely revered.[^cite_note-Rocca_2022-348][^cite_note-349] In 2015, 61.6% of respondents outlined that religion is of high or very high importance.[^cite_note-350] However, church attendance has greatly decreased in recent years; only 28% of Catholics attended [mass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_\(liturgy\)) weekly in 2021, down from around half in 2000.[^cite_note-351] According to *[The Wall Street Journal](173070.md)*, "Of \[the] more than 100 countries studied by the *[Pew Research Center](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pew_Research_Center)* in 2018, Poland was [secularising](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Secularization) the fastest, as measured by the disparity between the religiosity of young people and their elders."[^cite_note-Rocca_2022-348]&#x20;

Freedom of religion in Poland is guaranteed by the Constitution, and Poland's [concordat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concordat) with the [Holy See](13393.md) enables the teaching of religion in public schools.[^cite_note-352] Historically, the Polish state maintained a high degree of [religious tolerance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Confederation) and provided asylum for refugees fleeing religious persecution in other parts of Europe.[^cite_note-353] Poland hosted Europe's largest [Jewish diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Poland), and the country was a centre of [Ashkenazi Jewish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ashkenazi_Jewish) culture and traditional learning until the [Holocaust](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Holocaust).[^cite_note-354]&#x20;

Contemporary religious minorities include [Eastern Orthodox Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Orthodox_Church), [Protestants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Protestantism), including [Lutherans](23371382.md) of the [Evangelical-Augsburg Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelical-Augsburg_Church_in_Poland), [Pentecostals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostalism) in the [Pentecostal Church in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecostal_Church_in_Poland), [Adventists](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adventists) in the [Seventh-day Adventist Church](28632.md). Other smaller Christian denominations include [Eastern Catholics](214903.md), [Mariavites](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariavite_Church), [Evangelicalism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Evangelicalism) denominations. Other religions include [Jehovah's Witnesses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jehovah%27s_Witnesses), [Jews](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Poland), [Muslims](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Islam_in_Poland) ([Tatars](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tatars)), and [neopagans](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Modern_Paganism), some of whom are members of the [Native Polish Church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Native_Polish_Church).[^cite_note-Central_Statistical_Office_2008-355]&#x20;

### Health

Main article: [Health in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_in_Poland)

Medical service providers and [hospitals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hospitals) in Poland are subordinate to the [Ministry of Health](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_Health_of_the_Republic_of_Poland); it provides administrative oversight and scrutiny of general medical practice and is obliged to maintain a high standard of [hygiene](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hygiene) and patient care. Poland has a [universal healthcare system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universal_health_care) based on an all-inclusive [insurance system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Health_insurance); state subsidised healthcare is available to all citizens covered by the general health insurance programme of the [National Health Fund](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Health_Fund) (NFZ). Private medical complexes exist nationwide; over 50% of the population uses both public and private sectors.[^cite_note-356][^cite_note-357][^cite_note-justlanded-358]&#x20;

According to the [Human Development Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/HDI) from 2020, the average life expectancy at birth is 79 years (around 75 years for an infant male and 83 years for an infant female);[^cite_note-\:0-239] the country has a low [infant mortality rate](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Infant_mortality_rate) (4 per 1,000 births).[^cite_note-359] In 2019, Poland was the 15th-largest importer of [medications](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Medications) and pharmaceutical products.[^cite_note-360] In the same year, the principal cause of death was [ischemic heart disease](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ischemic_heart_disease); diseases of the [circulatory system](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Circulatory_system) accounted for 45% of all deaths.[^cite_note-361] Moreover, the increasing rate of childhood [obesity](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Obesity) in Poland is one of the fastest in Europe.[^cite_note-362] Health professionals warn that if these rapid upward trajectories continue, the current generation of children could be the first in modern history to have a shorter life expectancy than their parents due to obesity-related complications like [type 2 diabetes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Type_2_diabetes) and [hypertension](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hypertension).[^cite_note-363]&#x20;

### Education

Main articles: [Education in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Education_in_Poland) and [Universities in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Universities_in_Poland)



[Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University) in [Kraków](16815.md), one of the world's oldest institutions of higher learning

The [Jagiellonian University](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jagiellonian_University) founded in 1364 by [Casimir III](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Casimir_III_the_Great) in [Kraków](16815.md) was the first institution of higher learning established in Poland, and is one of the [oldest universities](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_oldest_universities_in_continuous_operation) still in continuous operation.[^cite_note-364] Poland's [Commission of National Education](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Commission_of_National_Education) (*Komisja Edukacji Narodowej*), established in 1773, was the world's first state ministry of education.[^cite_note-365][^cite_note-366] In 2018, the [Programme for International Student Assessment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Programme_for_International_Student_Assessment), coordinated by the [Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development), placed Poland's educational output as one of the highest in the OECD, ranking 5th by student attainment and 6th by student performance in 2022.[^cite_note-367][^cite_note-368]&#x20;

The framework for primary, secondary and higher tertiary education are established by the [Ministry of Education and Science](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ministry_of_National_Education_\(Poland\)). One year of kindergarten is [compulsory](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Compulsory_education) for six-year-olds.[^cite_note-369][^cite_note-Sejm_2016-370] Primary education traditionally begins at the age of seven, although children aged six can attend at the request of their parents or guardians.[^cite_note-Sejm_2016-370] Elementary school spans eight grades and secondary schooling is dependent on student preference – a four-year high school (*[liceum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/General_education_liceum)*), a five-year technical school (*[technikum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Technikum_\(Polish_education\))*) or various [vocational studies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vocational_school) (*szkoła branżowa*) can be pursued by individual pupils.[^cite_note-Sejm_2016-370] A liceum or technikum is concluded with a maturity exit exam (*[matura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matura)*), which must be passed in order to apply for a university or other institutions of higher learning.[^cite_note-371]&#x20;

In Poland, there are over 500 university-level institutions,[^cite_note-372] with numerous faculties.[^cite_note-373] The [University of Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Warsaw) and [Warsaw Polytechnic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warsaw_Polytechnic), the [University of Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Wroc%C5%82aw), [Adam Mickiewicz University in Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Mickiewicz_University_in_Pozna%C5%84) and the [University of Technology in Gdańsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gda%C5%84sk_University_of_Technology) are among the most prominent.[^cite_note-374] There are three conventional [academic degrees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_degree) in Poland – *[licencjat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Licentiate_\(degree\))* or *[inżynier](188874.md)* (first cycle), *[magister](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magister_\(degree\))* (second cycle) and *[doktor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/PhD)* (third cycle qualification).[^cite_note-OECD_2009-375]&#x20;

## Culture

Main article: [Culture of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Poland)



The [Polish White Eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coat_of_arms_of_Poland) is Poland's enduring [national and cultural symbol](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_symbols_of_Poland).

The culture of Poland is closely connected with its intricate 1,000-year [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_Poland), and forms an important constituent in the [Western civilisation](21208200.md).[^cite_note-376] The Poles take great pride in their national identity which is often associated with the colours white and red, and exuded by the expression *biało-czerwoni* ("whitereds").[^cite_note-377] National symbols, chiefly the crowned [white-tailed eagle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/White-tailed_eagle), are often visible on clothing, insignia and emblems.[^cite_note-378] The architectural monuments of great importance are protected by the [National Heritage Board of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/National_Heritage_Board_of_Poland).[^cite_note-379] Over 100 of the country's most significant tangible wonders were enlisted onto the [Historic Monuments Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_Historic_Monuments_\(Poland\)),[^cite_note-380] with further 18 being recognised by [UNESCO](21786641.md) as World Heritage Sites.[^cite_note-381]&#x20;

### Holidays and traditions

See also: [Christmas in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_in_Poland)



[All Saints' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Saints%27_Day) on 1 November is one of the most important public holidays in Poland.

There are 14 government-approved annual public holidays – New Year on 1 January, [Three Kings' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epiphany_\(holiday\)) on 6 January, [Easter Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Sunday) and [Easter Monday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Monday), [Labour Day](39353050.md) on 1 May, [Constitution Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/3_May_Constitution_Day) on 3 May, [Pentecost](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pentecost), [Corpus Christi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Feast_of_Corpus_Christi), [Feast of the Assumption](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Assumption_of_Mary) on 15 August, [All Saints' Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/All_Saints%27_Day) on 1 November, [Independence Day](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Independence_Day_\(Poland\)) on 11 November and Christmastide on 24, 25 and 26 December.[^cite_note-382]&#x20;

Particular traditions and superstitious customs observed in Poland are not found elsewhere in Europe. Though Christmas Eve (*[Wigilia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wigilia)*) had not been a public holiday until 2025,[^cite_note-383] it remains the most memorable day of the entire year. [Trees](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_trees) are decorated on 24 December, hay is placed under the tablecloth to resemble Jesus' [manger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Manger), [Christmas wafers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_wafer) (*opłatek*) are shared between gathered guests and a [twelve-dish meatless supper](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Twelve-dish_Christmas_Eve_supper) is served that same evening when the [first star](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Star_of_Bethlehem) appears.[^cite_note-384] An empty plate and seat are symbolically left at the table for an unexpected guest.[^cite_note-385] On occasion, [carolers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Christmas_carols) journey around smaller towns with a folk [Turoń](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Turo%C5%84) creature until the [Lent](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lent) period.[^cite_note-386]&#x20;

A widely popular [doughnut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/P%C4%85czki) and sweet pastry feast occurs on [Fat Thursday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fat_Thursday), usually 52 days prior to Easter.[^cite_note-Borodo_2020-387] [Eggs](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_egg) for [Holy Sunday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Sunday) are painted and placed in decorated [baskets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%9Awi%C4%99conka) that are previously blessed by clergymen in churches on [Easter Saturday](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Easter_Saturday). Easter Monday is celebrated with pagan *[dyngus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%9Amigus-dyngus)* festivities, where the youth is engaged in water fights.[^cite_note-388][^cite_note-Borodo_2020-387] Cemeteries and graves of the deceased are annually visited by family members on All Saints' Day; tombstones are cleaned as a sign of respect and candles are lit to honour the dead on an unprecedented scale.[^cite_note-389]&#x20;

### Music and dance

Main article: [Music of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Music_of_Poland)

Frédéric Chopin

[Frédéric Chopin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric_Chopin) was a renowned classical composer and virtuoso pianist.

Artur Rubinstein

[Artur Rubinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arthur_Rubinstein) was one of the greatest concert pianists of the 20th century.

Polish [classical music](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Classical_music) encompasses medieval and early-modern [liturgical tradition](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Liturgical_music), regional [folk tunes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folk_music), and a world-renowned [piano](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Piano) legacy.[^cite_note-390] Among the leading classical artists were [Frédéric Chopin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fr%C3%A9d%C3%A9ric_Chopin), [Artur Rubinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Artur_Rubinstein), [Ignacy Jan Paderewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacy_Jan_Paderewski), [Krzysztof Penderecki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krzysztof_Penderecki), [Henryk Wieniawski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henryk_Wieniawski), [Karol Szymanowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Karol_Szymanowski), [Witold Lutosławski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witold_Lutos%C5%82awski), and [Stanisław Moniuszko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Moniuszko). Poland maintains a lively and diverse music scene which recognises its own music and entertainment genres, such as [sung poetry](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sung_poetry) and [disco polo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Disco_polo).[^cite_note-391] [Jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz) and [metal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Metal_\(music_genre\)) genres are also particularly popular, and the country has become a major venue for large-scale music festivals, chief among which are the [Pol'and'Rock Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pol%27and%27Rock_Festival), [Open'er Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open%27er_Festival), [Opole Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Opole_Festival) and [Sopot Festival](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sopot_Festival).[^cite_note-392][^cite_note-393]&#x20;

The origins of Polish music can be traced to 13th-century [polyphonic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polyphony) compositions associated with the [Notre Dame School](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Notre_Dame_School).[^cite_note-Sturman-394] The anthemic melodies of *[Bogurodzica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bogurodzica)* and *[God Is Born](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/God_Is_Born)* may also date back to this period, however, the first notable composer, [Nicholas of Radom](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miko%C5%82aj_z_Radomia), lived in the 15th century.[^cite_note-Sturman-394][^cite_note-395] In the 16th century, [Venceslaus Samotulinus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Venceslaus_Samotulinus) and [Mikołaj Gomółka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Miko%C5%82aj_Gom%C3%B3%C5%82ka) gained recognition, and [Diomedes Cato](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Diomedes_Cato), an Italian [lutenist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lute) at the court of Sigismund III, imported musical styles from [southern Europe](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Southern_Europe).[^cite_note-The_Music_Courts_of_the_Polish_Vasas-396] At the end of the 18th century, Polish classical music evolved into national forms, and [Wojciech Bogusławski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wojciech_Bogus%C5%82awski) is credited for composing the first Polish national opera in 1794.[^cite_note-397] *[Halka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Halka)*, *[The Haunted Manor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Haunted_Manor_\(opera\))*, and *[King Roger](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/King_Roger_\(opera\))* are canons of 19th and early 20th-century [Polish opera](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_opera).[^cite_note-398] The [interwar period](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Interwar_period) of 1918–1939 saw the rise of [jazz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jazz), [foxtrot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Foxtrot), Polish [tango](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tango) and a strong [cabaret](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cabaret) culture.[^cite_note-399]&#x20;

Polish choreography is a sophisticated ethnographic system comprising five [national folk dances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_folk_dances); the [polonaise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonaise), [mazurka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mazurka), [krakowiak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krakowiak) (cracovienne), [kujawiak](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kujawiak), and [oberek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oberek) as well as various types of [polkas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polka).[^cite_note-400] These are performed in national costumes for larger cultural events and accompanied by instruments such as the [fiddle](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fiddle), [bass](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Double_bass), [clarinet](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Clarinet) and [accordion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Accordion) across much of Poland, and by the highlander [bagpipes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagpipes) in the southern mountainous areas.[^cite_note-401] Since 2023, the polonaise has been inscribed in the [UNESCO Intangible Cultural Heritage List](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UNESCO_Intangible_Cultural_Heritage_List).[^cite_note-402] Polish dances also spread across much of the [Nordic countries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nordic_countries) and developed into the [polska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polska_\(dance\)) family of music and dance.[^cite_note-403]&#x20;

### Art

Main articles: [Art in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_in_Poland) and [Young Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Poland)

Jan Matejko

[Jan Matejko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Matejko), leading Polish [history painter](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_painting) whose works depict Poland's heritage and key historical events

Lady with an Ermine

*[Lady with an Ermine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_with_an_Ermine)* (1490) by [Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci) is displayed in the [Czartoryski Museum](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czartoryski_Museum) in Kraków.

Art in Poland has invariably reflected [European](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_of_Europe) trends, with Polish painting pivoted on [folklore](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Folklore), [Catholic themes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Catholic_art), [historicism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_painting) and [realism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Realism_\(arts\)), but also on [Impressionism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism) and [romanticism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism_in_Poland). An important art movement was [Young Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Young_Poland), developed in the late 19th century for promoting [decadence](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Decadent_movement), [symbolism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Symbolism_\(arts\)) and [Art Nouveau](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Nouveau). Since the 20th century Polish documentary art and photography has enjoyed worldwide fame, especially the [Polish School of Posters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_School_of_Posters).[^cite_note-MFA-404] One of the most distinguished paintings in Poland is *[Lady with an Ermine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lady_with_an_Ermine)* (1490) by [Leonardo da Vinci](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Leonardo_da_Vinci).[^cite_note-405]&#x20;

Internationally renowned Polish artists include [Jan Matejko](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Matejko) (historicism), [Jacek Malczewski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jacek_Malczewski) (symbolism), [Stanisław Wyspiański](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Wyspia%C5%84ski) (art nouveau), [Henryk Siemiradzki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henryk_Siemiradzki) (Roman [academic art](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academic_art)), [Tamara de Lempicka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tamara_de_Lempicka) ([art deco](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_deco)), and [Zdzisław Beksiński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zdzis%C5%82aw_Beksi%C5%84ski) (dystopian [surrealism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Surrealism)).[^cite_note-406] Several Polish artists and sculptors were also acclaimed representatives of [avant-garde](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Avant-garde), [constructivist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Constructivism_\(art\)), [minimalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Minimalism) and contemporary art movements, including [Katarzyna Kobro](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katarzyna_Kobro), [Władysław Strzemiński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_Strzemi%C5%84ski), [Magdalena Abakanowicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdalena_Abakanowicz), [Alina Szapocznikow](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Alina_Szapocznikow), [Igor Mitoraj](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Igor_Mitoraj) and [Wilhelm Sasnal](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wilhelm_Sasnal).&#x20;

Notable art academies in Poland include the [Kraków Academy of Fine Arts](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krak%C3%B3w_Academy_of_Fine_Arts), [Academy of Fine Arts in Warsaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Academy_of_Fine_Arts_in_Warsaw), [Art Academy of Szczecin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Art_Academy_of_Szczecin), [University of Fine Arts in Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/University_of_Fine_Arts_in_Pozna%C5%84) and the [Geppert Academy of Fine Arts in Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eugeniusz_Geppert_Academy_of_Fine_Arts). Contemporary works are exhibited at [Zachęta](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zach%C4%99ta), [Ujazdów](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ujazd%C3%B3w_Castle), and [MOCAK](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/MOCAK,_Museum_of_Contemporary_Art_in_Krak%C3%B3w) art galleries.[^cite_note-407]&#x20;

### Architecture

Main article: [Architecture of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Poland)

Saint Mary's Church in Kraków

[St. Mary's Basilica](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary%27s_Basilica,_Krak%C3%B3w) on the [Main Market Square](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Main_Market_Square,_Krak%C3%B3w) in [Kraków](16815.md) is an example of [Brick Gothic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brick_Gothic) architecture.

Poznań City Hall

The 16th-century [City Hall](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84_Town_Hall) of [Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84) illustrates the [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_in_Poland) style.

The [architecture of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Poland) reflects [European architectural](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Europe) styles, with strong historical influences derived from [Italy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Italy), [Germany](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Architecture_of_Germany), and the [Low Countries](51402.md).[^cite_note-408] Settlements founded on [Magdeburg Law](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Magdeburg_rights) evolved around [central marketplaces](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Town_square) (*plac*, *rynek*), encircled by a grid or [concentric](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Concentric_objects) network of streets forming an [old town](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_town) (*stare miasto*).[^cite_note-409] Poland's traditional landscape is characterised by ornate churches, [city tenements](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kamienica_\(architecture\)) and [town halls](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ratusz).[^cite_note-410] [Cloth hall markets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cloth_hall) (*sukiennice*) were once an abundant feature of Polish urban architecture.[^cite_note-411] The mountainous south is known for its [Zakopane chalet style](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zakopane_Style), which originated in Poland.[^cite_note-412]&#x20;

The earliest architectonic trend was [Romanesque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanesque_architecture) (c. 11th century), but its traces in the form of [circular rotundas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rotunda_\(architecture\)) are scarce.[^cite_note-413] The arrival of [brick Gothic](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brick_Gothic) (c. 13th century) defined Poland's most distinguishable medieval style, exuded by the castles of [Malbork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Malbork_Castle), [Lidzbark](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lidzbark_Castle), [Gniew](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gniew_Castle) and [Kwidzyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kwidzyn_Castle) as well as the cathedrals of [Gniezno](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gniezno_Cathedral), [Gdańsk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary%27s_Church,_Gda%C5%84sk), [Wrocław](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw_Cathedral), [Frombork](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Frombork_Cathedral) and [Kraków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/St._Mary%27s_Basilica,_Krak%C3%B3w).[^cite_note-414] The [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_in_Poland) (16th century) gave rise to Italianate courtyards, defensive [palazzos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Palazzo) and [mausoleums](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sigismund%27s_Chapel).[^cite_note-415] Decorative [attics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Attic_\(architecture\)) with [pinnacles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pinnacle) and [arcade](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Arcade_\(architecture\)) [loggias](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Loggia) are elements of [Polish Mannerism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mannerist_architecture), found in [Poznań](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pozna%C5%84), [Lublin](74594.md) and [Zamość](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zamo%C5%9B%C4%87).[^cite_note-416][^cite_note-Mikos-417] Foreign artisans often came at the expense of kings or nobles, whose palaces were built thereafter in the [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Baroque_in_Poland), [Neoclassical](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Neoclassical_architecture_in_Poland) and [Revivalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Revivalism_\(architecture\)) styles (17th–19th century).[^cite_note-418]&#x20;

Primary building materials [timber](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Lumber) and [red brick](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Brick) were used extensively in Polish folk architecture,[^cite_note-419] and the concept of a [fortified church](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Fortified_church) was commonplace.[^cite_note-420] Secular structures such as [*dworek* manor houses](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Dw%C3%B3r_\(manor_house\)), [farmsteads](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Farmhouses), [granaries](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Granary), [mills](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Gristmill) and country [inns](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inn) are still present in some regions or in open air museums (*[skansen](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open-Air_Museum)*).[^cite_note-421] However, traditional construction methods faded in the early-mid 20th century due to urbanisation and the construction of [functionalist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Functionalist_architecture) [housing estates](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Housing_estate) and [residential areas](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Residential_architecture_in_Poland).[^cite_note-422]&#x20;

### Literature

Main articles: [Polish literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_literature) and [History of philosophy in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/History_of_philosophy_in_Poland)

Adam Mickiewicz

[Adam Mickiewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Mickiewicz), whose [national epic poem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Epic_poetry) *[Pan Tadeusz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_Tadeusz)* (1834) is considered a masterpiece of [Polish literature](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_literature)

Joseph Conrad-Korzeniowski

[Joseph Conrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Conrad), author of popular books such as *[Heart of Darkness](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Heart_of_Darkness)* (1899) and *[Nostromo](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nostromo)* (1904)

The [literary works of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_literature) have traditionally concentrated around the themes of patriotism, [spirituality](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Spirituality), social [allegories](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Allegory) and moral narratives.[^cite_note-423] The earliest examples of Polish literature, written in [Latin](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Latin), date to the 12th century.[^cite_note-Koca_2006-424] The first [Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_language) phrase *Day ut ia pobrusa, a ti poziwai* (officially translated as "Let me, I shall grind, and you take a rest") was documented in the [Book of Henryków](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Book_of_Henryk%C3%B3w) and reflected the use of a [quern-stone](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quern-stone).[^cite_note-425] It has been since included in [UNESCO's Memory of World Register](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Memory_of_the_World_Programme).[^cite_note-426] The oldest extant manuscripts of fine [prose](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Prose) in [Old Polish](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Old_Polish_language) are the [Holy Cross Sermons](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Holy_Cross_Sermons) and the [Bible of Queen Sophia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bible_of_Queen_Sophia),[^cite_note-427] and [Calendarium cracoviense](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Almanach_cracoviense_ad_annum_1474) (1474) is Poland's oldest surviving [print](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Printing_press).[^cite_note-428]&#x20;

The poets [Jan Kochanowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Kochanowski) and [Nicholas Rey](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Nicholas_Rey) became the first [Renaissance](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Renaissance_in_Poland) authors to write in Polish.[^cite_note-429] Prime literarians of the period included [Dantiscus](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Johannes_Dantiscus), [Modrevius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andreus_Fricius_Modrevius), [Goslicius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wawrzyniec_Go%C5%9Blicki), [Sarbievius](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Matthias_Sarbievius) and theologian [John Laski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/John_Laski). In the [Baroque](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Baroque) era, [Jesuit philosophy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jesuits) and local culture greatly influenced the literary techniques of [Jan Andrzej Morsztyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Andrzej_Morsztyn) ([Marinism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Marinism)) and [Jan Chryzostom Pasek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Chryzostom_Pasek) ([sarmatian](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarmatism) memoirs).[^cite_note-430] During the [Enlightenment](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_Enlightenment), playwright [Ignacy Krasicki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ignacy_Krasicki) composed the first Polish-language [novel](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Adventures_of_Mr._Nicholas_Wisdom).[^cite_note-431] Poland's leading 19th-century [romantic poets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Romanticism_in_Poland) were the [Three Bards](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Three_Bards) – [Juliusz Słowacki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliusz_S%C5%82owacki), [Zygmunt Krasiński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Zygmunt_Krasi%C5%84ski) and [Adam Mickiewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Adam_Mickiewicz), whose epic poem *[Pan Tadeusz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pan_Tadeusz)* (1834) is a national classic.[^cite_note-432] In the 20th century, the English [impressionist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Impressionism_\(literature\)) and early [modernist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Literary_modernism) writings of [Joseph Conrad](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Joseph_Conrad) made him one of the most eminent novelists of all time.[^cite_note-433][^cite_note-434]&#x20;

Contemporary Polish literature is versatile, with its fantasy genre having been particularly praised.[^cite_note-435] The philosophical [sci-fi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Science-fiction) novel *[Solaris](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Solaris_\(novel\))* by [Stanisław Lem](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Lem) and *[The Witcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Witcher)* series by [Andrzej Sapkowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Sapkowski) are celebrated works of world fiction.[^cite_note-436] Poland has six [Nobel-Prize winning](23385442.md) authors – [Henryk Sienkiewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Henryk_Sienkiewicz) (*[Quo Vadis](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Quo_Vadis_\(novel\))*; 1905), [Władysław Reymont](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/W%C5%82adys%C5%82aw_Reymont) (*[The Peasants](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Peasants)*; 1924), [Isaac Bashevis Singer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Isaac_Bashevis_Singer) (1978), [Czesław Miłosz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Czes%C5%82aw_Mi%C5%82osz) (1980), [Wisława Szymborska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wis%C5%82awa_Szymborska) (1996), and [Olga Tokarczuk](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olga_Tokarczuk) (2018).[^cite_note-Facts_on_the_Nobel_Prize_in_Literature-437][^cite_note-Gopnik_2007-438][^cite_note-439]&#x20;

### Cuisine

Main article: [Polish cuisine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_cuisine)



[Kielbasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kielbasa) sausage, a staple of Polish cuisine, with [pickled cucumbers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pickled_cucumber) and [rye bread](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Rye_bread)

The cuisine of Poland is eclectic, with many regional varieties, sharing some similarity with other neighbouring cuisines.[^cite_note-Puri-440] Among the staple or regional dishes are [pierogi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pierogi) (filled dumplings), [kielbasa](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kielbasa) (sausage), [bigos](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bigos) (hunter's stew), [kotlet schabowy](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kotlet_schabowy) (breaded cutlet), [gołąbki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Go%C5%82%C4%85bki) (cabbage rolls), [barszcz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Borscht) (borscht), [żurek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%BBurek) (soured rye soup), [oscypek](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Oscypek) (smoked cheese), and [tomato soup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tomato_soup).[^cite_note-Marc_Heine-441][^cite_note-Strybel_2003-442] [Bagels](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bagel), a type of [bread roll](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bread_roll), also originated in Poland.[^cite_note-443]&#x20;

Traditional dishes are hearty and abundant in pork, potatoes, eggs, cream, mushrooms, regional herbs, and sauce.[^cite_note-444] Polish food is characteristic for its various kinds of [kluski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kluski) (soft dumplings), [soups](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Soup), cereals and a variety of breads and [open sandwiches](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Open_sandwich). Salads, including [mizeria](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mizeria) (cucumber salad), [coleslaw](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Coleslaw), [sauerkraut](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sauerkraut), carrot and [seared beets](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Searing), are common. Meals conclude with a dessert such as [sernik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cheesecake) (cheesecake), [makowiec](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Makowiec_\(pastry\)) (poppy seed roll), or [napoleonka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Napoleonka) ([mille-feuille](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mille-feuille)) cream pie.[^cite_note-445]&#x20;

Traditional alcoholic beverages include honey [mead](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mead_in_Poland), widespread since the 13th century, [beer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Beer_in_Poland), wine and [vodka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Vodka).[^cite_note-446] The world's first written mention of vodka originates from Poland.[^cite_note-447] The most popular alcoholic drinks at present are beer and wine which took over from vodka more popular in the years 1980–1998.[^cite_note-beverages-448] [Grodziskie](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Grodziskie), sometimes referred to as "Polish Champagne", is an example of a historical beer style from Poland.[^cite_note-449] Tea remains common in Polish society since the 19th century, whilst coffee is drunk widely since the 18th century.[^cite_note-450]&#x20;

### Fashion and design

Further information: [Category\:Polish fashion](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Category\:Polish_fashion)



Traditional *[polonaise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonaise_\(clothing\))* dresses, 1780–1785

Several Polish designers and stylists left a legacy of beauty inventions and cosmetics; including [Helena Rubinstein](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Helena_Rubinstein) and [Maksymilian Faktorowicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Maksymilian_Faktorowicz), who created a line of cosmetics company in California known as [Max Factor](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Factor) and formulated the term "make-up" which is now widely used as an alternative for describing cosmetics.[^cite_note-451] Faktorowicz is also credited with inventing modern [eyelash extensions](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eyelash_extensions).[^cite_note-452][^cite_note-453] As of 2020, Poland possesses the sixth-largest cosmetic market in Europe. [Inglot Cosmetics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Inglot_Cosmetics) is the country's largest beauty products manufacturer,[^cite_note-454] and the retail store [Reserved](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Reserved) is the country's most successful clothing store chain.[^cite_note-455]&#x20;

Historically, fashion has been an important aspect of Poland's national consciousness or [cultural manifestation](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Culture_of_Poland), beginning with the [poulaine](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poulaine) pointed footwear in the 14th century, to developing an endemic style known as [Sarmatism](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sarmatism) at the turn of the 17th century.[^cite_note-Biedronska-Slota_2005-456] The national dress and etiquette of Poland reached the court at [Versailles](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Versailles), where French dresses inspired by Polish garments included *[robe à la polonaise](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polonaise_\(clothing\))* and the [witzchoura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Witzchoura). The scope of influence also entailed furniture; rococo [Polish beds](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_bed) with [canopies](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Canopy_bed) became fashionable in châteaus.[^cite_note-457] Sarmatism eventually faded in the wake of the 18th century.[^cite_note-Biedronska-Slota_2005-456]&#x20;

### Cinema

Main article: [Cinema of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Poland)



[Andrzej Wajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Wajda) (1926–2016), renowned Polish film director

The [cinema of Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinema_of_Poland) traces its origins to 1894, when inventor [Kazimierz Prószyński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazimierz_Pr%C3%B3szy%C5%84ski) patented the [Pleograph](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pleograph) and subsequently the [Aeroscope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Aeroscope), the first successful hand-held operated film camera.[^cite_note-Ford_2009-458][^cite_note-Haltof_2015-459] In 1897, [Jan Szczepanik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Szczepanik) constructed the [Telectroscope](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telectroscope), a prototype of television transmitting images and sounds.[^cite_note-Ford_2009-458] They are both recognised as pioneers of [cinematography](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cinematography).[^cite_note-Ford_2009-458] Poland has also produced influential directors, film producers and actors, many of whom were active in [Hollywood](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hollywood,_Los_Angeles), chiefly [Roman Polański](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Roman_Pola%C5%84ski), [Andrzej Wajda](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Andrzej_Wajda), [Pola Negri](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pola_Negri), [Samuel Goldwyn](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Samuel_Goldwyn), the [Warner brothers](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Warner_Brothers), [Max Fleischer](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Max_Fleischer), [Agnieszka Holland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnieszka_Holland), [Krzysztof Zanussi](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krzysztof_Zanussi) and [Krzysztof Kieślowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krzysztof_Kie%C5%9Blowski).[^cite_note-460]&#x20;

The [themes](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_genre) commonly explored in Polish cinema include [history](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Historical_film), [drama](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Drama_\(film_and_television\)), war, culture and black realism ([film noir](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Film_noir)).[^cite_note-Ford_2009-458][^cite_note-Haltof_2015-459] In the 21st-century, two Polish productions won the [Academy Awards](324.md) – *[The Pianist](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Pianist_\(2002_film\))* (2002) by Roman Polański and *[Ida](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ida_\(film\))* (2013) by [Paweł Pawlikowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Pawe%C5%82_Pawlikowski).[^cite_note-Haltof_2015-459] Polish cinematography also created many well-received comedies. The most known of them were made by [Stanisław Bareja](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Stanis%C5%82aw_Bareja) and [Juliusz Machulski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Juliusz_Machulski).&#x20;

### Media

Main article: [Mass media in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mass_media_in_Poland)



Headquarters of the publicly funded national television network [TVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telewizja_Polska) in Warsaw

According to the [Eurobarometer Report](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eurobarometer) (2015), 78 percent of Poles watch the [television](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Television_in_Poland) daily.[^cite_note-Stepinska_2020-461] In 2020, 79 percent of the population read the news more than once a day, placing it second behind Sweden.[^cite_note-462] Poland has a number of major domestic media outlets, chiefly the [public broadcasting](178808.md) corporation [TVP](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Telewizja_Polska), [free-to-air](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Free-to-air) channels [TVN](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVN_\(Polish_TV_channel\)) and [Polsat](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polsat) as well as 24-hour news channels [TVP Info](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Info), [TVN 24](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVN_24) and [Polsat News](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polsat_News).[^cite_note-463] Public television extends its operations to genre-specific programmes such as [TVP Sport](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Sport), [TVP Historia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Historia), [TVP Kultura](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Kultura), [TVP Rozrywka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Rozrywka), TVP Seriale and [TVP Polonia](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/TVP_Polonia), the latter a state-run channel dedicated to the transmission of Polish-language telecasts for the [Polish diaspora](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Polish_diaspora). In 2020, the most popular types of newspapers were [tabloids](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Tabloid_\(newspaper_format\)) and socio-political news dailies.[^cite_note-Stepinska_2020-461]&#x20;

Poland is a major European hub for video game developers and among the most successful companies are [CD Projekt](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CD_Projekt), [Techland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Techland), [The Farm 51](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Farm_51), [CI Games](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/CI_Games) and [People Can Fly](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/People_Can_Fly).[^cite_note-Marszałkowski_2021-464] Some of the popular video games developed in Poland include *[The Witcher](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/The_Witcher_\(video_game_series\))* trilogy and *[Cyberpunk 2077](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cyberpunk_2077)*.[^cite_note-Marszałkowski_2021-464] The Polish city of [Katowice](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Katowice) also hosts [Intel Extreme Masters](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Intel_Extreme_Masters), one of the biggest [esports](564204.md) events in the world.[^cite_note-Marszałkowski_2021-464]&#x20;

### Sports

Main articles: [Sport in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Sport_in_Poland) and [Poland at the Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_at_the_Olympics)



The [Kazimierz Górski National Stadium](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Kazimierz_G%C3%B3rski_National_Stadium) in Warsaw, home of the [national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_national_football_team)

[Motorcycle speedway](230182.md), volleyball and association football are among the country's most popular sports, with a rich history of international competitions.[^cite_note-FIFA_World_Cup-465][^cite_note-FIFA_Statistics-466] [Track and field](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Track_and_field_athletics), basketball, [handball](13730.md), boxing, [MMA](228344.md), [ski jumping](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Ski_jumping), [cross-country skiing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Cross-country_skiing_\(sport\)), [ice hockey](14790.md), tennis, fencing, swimming, and [weightlifting](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Olympic_weightlifting) are other popular sports. The golden era of [football in Poland](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_in_Poland) occurred throughout the 1970s and went on until the early 1980s when the [Polish national football team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_national_football_team) achieved their best results in any FIFA World Cup competitions finishing third place in [the 1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIFA_World_Cup) and [the 1982](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1982_FIFA_World_Cup) tournaments. The team won a gold medal [in football](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Football_at_the_Summer_Olympics) at the [1972 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1972_Summer_Olympics) and two silver medals, [in 1976](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1976_Summer_Olympics) and [in 1992](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1992_Summer_Olympics). In 2012, Poland co-hosted the [UEFA European Football Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/UEFA_Euro_2012).[^cite_note-warsaw-life-467]&#x20;

As of September 2024, the [Polish men's national volleyball team](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Poland_men%27s_national_volleyball_team) is ranked [as first](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIVB_World_Rankings) in the world.[^cite_note-468] The team won a gold medal at the [1976 Summer Olympics](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Volleyball_at_the_1976_Summer_Olympics_%E2%80%93_Men%27s_tournament) and the gold medal at the [FIVB World Championship](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/FIVB_Volleyball_Men%27s_World_Championship) [1974](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/1974_FIVB_Volleyball_Men%27s_World_Championship), [2014](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2014_FIVB_Volleyball_Men%27s_World_Championship) and [2018](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2018_FIVB_Volleyball_Men%27s_World_Championship).[^cite_note-469][^cite_note-470] [Mariusz Pudzianowski](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Mariusz_Pudzianowski) is a highly successful strongman competitor and has won more [World's Strongest Man](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/World%27s_Strongest_Man) titles than any other competitor in the world, winning the event in 2008 for the fifth time.[^cite_note-471]&#x20;

Poland has made a distinctive mark [in motorcycle speedway racing](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway_in_Poland). The top [Ekstraliga](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway_Ekstraliga) division has one of [the highest average attendances](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/List_of_attendance_figures_at_domestic_professional_sports_leagues#Outdoor_sports) for any sport in Poland. The [national speedway team of Poland](12546053.md) is one of the major teams in international speedway. Individually, Poland has three [Speedway Grand Prix](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Speedway_Grand_Prix) World Champions, with the most successful being five-time World Champion [Bartosz Zmarzlik](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Bartosz_Zmarzlik) who won back-to-back championships in 2019 and 2020 as well as 2022, 2023 and 2024. In 2021, Poland finished runners-up in the Speedway of Nations world championship final, held in [Manchester](20206.md), England in 2021.[^cite_note-Speedway-472]&#x20;

In the 21st century, the country has seen a growth of popularity of tennis and produced a number of successful tennis players including World No. 1 [Iga Świątek](51838558.md), winner of five [Grand Slam](197638.md) singles titles; former World No. 2 [Agnieszka Radwanska](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Agnieszka_Radwanska), winner of 20 WTA career singles titles including [2015 WTA Finals](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_WTA_Finals_%E2%80%93_Singles); Top 10 ATP player [Hubert Hurkacz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Hubert_Hurkacz); former World No. 1 doubles player [Łukasz Kubot](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/%C5%81ukasz_Kubot), winner of two Grand Slam doubles titles and [Jan Zieliński](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jan_Zieli%C5%84ski), winner of two Grand Slam mixed doubles titles. Poland also won the [2015 Hopman Cup](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/2015_Hopman_Cup) with Agnieszka Radwańska and [Jerzy Janowicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerzy_Janowicz) representing the country.[^cite_note-473][^cite_note-474]&#x20;

Poles made significant achievements in mountaineering, in particular, in the [Himalayas](37966.md) and the winter ascending of the [eight-thousanders](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Eight-thousander) (e.g. [Jerzy Kukuczka](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Jerzy_Kukuczka), [Krzysztof Wielicki](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Krzysztof_Wielicki), [Wanda Rutkiewicz](https://en.wikipedia.org/wiki/Wanda_Rutkiewicz)). Polish mountains are one of the tourist attractions of the country. Hiking, climbing, skiing and mountain biking and attract numerous tourists every year from all over the world.[^cite_note-UNTWO_2008-303] Water sports are the most popular summer recreation activities, with ample locations for fishing, canoeing, kayaking, sailing and windsurfing especially in the northern regions of the country.[^cite_note-475]&#x20;
